Grepped every file under `lib/features/play` for `Semantics(` and cross-referenced against the full 66-file arcade dir listing: the stale batch-193 note ("only dot_to_dot/firefly_count/letter_trace/mirror_draw/star_path have it") was wrong — 17 of 66 arcade files already have Semantics coverage (those 5 plus `balloon_bounce`, `balloon_math`, `beat_builder`, `bubble_shooter`, `bubble_wrap`, `bug_catch`, `catch_beat`, `count_pop`, `piano_tiles`, `shape_builder`, `shape_sort_chute`, `slide_puzzle`, added across earlier batches never recorded under this exact lens name), but 49 arcade files plus goal_games/mini_games' non-Semantics widgets still have zero screen-reader exposure. Picked the clearest concrete case from that list: `odd_one_out_game.dart` — a 2x2-to-5x5 grid of bare `CustomPaint` cells (shape+colour swatches) tappable only via raw `onTapDown` coordinate math, with literally no Semantics tree at all, making this entirely-visual "spot the different tile" game silently unplayable by a blind child (unlike every other grid/choice game already swept). Fixed by adding a per-tile Semantics overlay (one per cell, positioned to match the grid maths already used by the `GestureDetector`) with a `label` describing row/column + colour name + shape name (e.g. "Row 2 column 3: red triangle"), wired to the same `_tap(i)` handler via `onTap` — critically, the label describes every tile's own appearance, never flags which one is "the odd one", so a screen-reader user must compare announced descriptions themselves exactly as a sighted child compares visually, preserving the actual gameplay challenge rather than trivializing it. Added `_colorName`/`_shapeNames` helpers (nearest-palette-match + light/dark shade detection, since the odd tile's colour is a subtly shifted lightness of the same hue, not a different palette colour). Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 4d29615. NEXT_ACTION for batch 195: continue the Semantics sweep — 48 arcade files still lack any Semantics (full list is reproducible via `Get-ChildItem lib/features/play/toys/arcade/*.dart | Select-String 'Semantics\(' -List` diffed against the full file listing); best next targets are other discrete-choice/matching games where screen-reader description would restore real playability without trivializing the challenge: `pattern_weaver_game.dart`, `shadow_match_game.dart`, `sorting_train_game.dart`, `weather_sort_game.dart`, `tap_order_game.dart`, `feelings_match_game.dart`, `kindness_match_game.dart`, `tone_match_game.dart`, `tic_tac_toe_game.dart`, `memory_flip_game.dart`, `memory_pairs_deluxe_game.dart`, `ball_sort_game.dart`, `jigsaw_four_game.dart`, `calm_choices_game.dart`. Continuous-physics/reflex games (`air_hockey`, `pinball`, `brick_break`, `space_dodge`, `sky_hop`, `whack`, `stack`, `skee_ball`, `basketball`, `soccer_kick`, `penalty_dash`, `hoop_toss`, `fishing`, `maze_marble`, `maze_run`, `balance_ball`, `steady_hand`, `quick_tap`, `echo_game`, `rhythm_clap`, `echo_drums`, `drum_garden`, `xylophone_tap`, `piano_song`, `bigger_number`, `add_it_up`, `counting_baskets`, `spot_difference`, `block_blast`, `merge_game`, `mini_golf`) are a much weaker fit for discrete per-tile Semantics (continuous drag/timing-based, not a fixed set of nameable targets) — treat those as lower priority or needing a different accessibility approach (e.g. audio cues, which most already have) rather than forcing a tile-grid pattern onto them. Once the arcade sweep is exhausted, extend the same lens to `goal_games.dart`/`mini_games.dart`'s non-Semantics games. If a full fresh sweep comes back with no further concrete wins, pivot to Phase 11 Definition-of-Done section 9 (device QA checklist, final honest product review) item by item instead of idling; do not declare FACTORY_COMPLETE without explicitly working or recording BLOCKED status on each of its 10 items.
PRIOR_BATCH_192:   Batch 192: followed batch 191's NEXT_ACTION, reading `echo_drums_game.dart`, `add_it_up_game.dart`, `steady_hand_game.dart` fresh end-to-end for the flat-speed/flat-timing bug class. `add_it_up` and `steady_hand` came back genuinely clean — `add_it_up`'s `_maxVal`/`_tileCount` already ramp with `_score` across the whole 10-round game (a prior batch's fix, confirmed still in place), and `steady_hand`'s 3 levels already narrow the corridor (`_widths = [0.08, 0.065, 0.05]`) plus use genuinely different path shapes, a real per-level escalation. Found one real, concrete gap in `echo_drums_game.dart`: it is a Simon-style memory game whose only escalation was sequence length (1 to 8 notes) — the per-note playback pace itself (`_timer = 0.5` show / `0.18` gap) was a flat constant at every round, so round 1's single-note echo played at literally the same tempo as round 8's eight-note echo, unlike every other round-based game in the catalog (`balloon_math`'s drift speed, `target_toss`'s ring speed, `add_it_up`'s own just-reconfirmed ramp) which escalate a real pace dimension, not just a count. Fixed by computing `_showDur`/`_gapDur` once per round in `_startShow()`, scaled down from 0.5s/0.18s at round 1 toward a 0.3s/0.1s floor by round 8 (`progress = _seq.length / _winLen`), so the memorised echo is genuinely faster — and harder to track — on later rounds, not just longer. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 79739c4.
PRIOR_BATCH_191:   Batch 191: followed batch 190's NEXT_ACTION, re-reading all 6 named candidates fresh end-to-end against the colour-match-only lens — `balloon_math_game.dart`, `count_pop_game.dart`, `target_toss_game.dart`, `tone_match_game.dart`, `feelings_match_game.dart`, `kindness_match_game.dart`. All 6 came back genuinely clean and none are false "already safe" claims this time: `balloon_math`'s balloons carry a colour but the real discriminator a child must read is the printed number, never colour alone; `count_pop`'s bubbles have random decorative hue but the task is counting, not colour-matching; `target_toss`'s ring colours are a fixed decorative archery-target palette, not a matching mechanic; `tone_match`'s core mechanic is explicitly auditory (bells look identical by design; colour only flashes briefly on reveal, never required to distinguish two simultaneously-visible options); `feelings_match`/`kindness_match` both distinguish options by emoji/text, never colour. Lens genuinely exhausted this time. Per CLAUDE.md section 7, widened the search within the same 6 files for any other concrete gameplay-feel gap and found one: `balloon_math_game.dart`'s balloon drift speed was a flat constant (`dt * 0.12`) for all 10 rounds — unlike `target_toss`'s ring speed (`_tSpeed = 0.18 + (_score/_target)*0.26`) or `count_pop`'s rising count floor, both of which escalate with score, Balloon Math had zero challenge-curve ramp; round 9 felt exactly as easy to catch as round 1. Fixed by ramping drift speed with `_score` (`0.12 + (_score/_target)*0.09`, capped at the win threshold), so the later rounds genuinely demand faster reading/tapping of the sum, matching the escalation convention already established catalog-wide. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: c7d7ae6.
PRIOR_BATCH_190:   Batch 190: followed batch 189's NEXT_ACTION, reading all 9 named candidates fresh end-to-end against the "nearest dot/target regardless of order" bug class — `air_hockey_game.dart`, `skee_ball_game.dart`, `block_blast_game.dart`, `merge_game.dart`, `ball_sort_game.dart`, `balance_ball_game.dart`, `xylophone_tap_game.dart`, `rhythm_clap_game.dart`, `steady_hand_game.dart`. All 9 came back genuinely clean — none of them have the multi-unordered-target shape (air_hockey/balance_ball/steady_hand are continuous-physics not discrete-target; block_blast/merge/ball_sort are grid-placement games with no "which of several same-state targets is next" ambiguity; xylophone_tap already enforces a single `_nextBar` target; rhythm_clap's markers are strictly time-ordered and its tight ±0.06 window plus earliest-unresolved-marker scan is the correct temporal-matching behaviour, not the bug class). Per CLAUDE.md section 7 (every launch must ship a real `lib/` improvement, not just a clean sweep), widened the search and found one in `bubble_shooter_game.dart`: batch 188's note had incorrectly logged it as already having colour-blind shape redundancy, but a fresh read showed its 5-colour palette (red/yellow/teal/sky/violet) relied purely on hue — no shape glyph — unlike every other match-by-colour game in the catalog (`pattern_weaver`, `sorting_train`, `shadow_match`, `odd_one_out`, `shape_sort_chute`), all of which already pair colour with a distinct shape via the shared `_paintPolyShape` helper so matching never depends on colour perception alone. Fixed by adding a `_shapeForColor` map (square/triangle/star/heart/diamond, skipping circle since every bubble is already a circle) and overlaying the matching glyph via `_paintPolyShape` on every grid, flying, launcher, and next-preview bubble in `_BubblePainter._ball`. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 1ba0855.
PRIOR_BATCH_189:   Batch 189: followed batch 188's NEXT_ACTION item (a), picking `mirror_draw_game.dart` as the top candidate (flagged as most likely to share `letter_trace`'s "nearest dot regardless of order" bug class). Read it fresh end-to-end and confirmed exactly that gap: `_touch` lit whichever unlit left-half dot was globally nearest the touch point, with zero check that it was next in the polyline's build order — the same "trace order isn't enforced" bug just fixed in `letter_trace_game.dart`, and just as fatal to this game's own "trace the dots on the left" pitch (a quick diagonal swipe could light dots scattered across the symmetric picture out of sequence, so a child could "finish" a picture without ever drawing its real shape). Fixed with the identical pattern: added a `_nextDotIdx` getter (first unlit dot in `_dots`, already built point-by-point in path order inside `_newShape`), made `_touch` compare distance only against that one enforced target, updated the Semantics overlay to expose only that next dot (was previously one overlay per every remaining unlit dot), and added a highlighted white ring for the next-target dot in `_MirrorDrawPainter` so the enforced order is visibly discoverable. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: a9f142d.
PRIOR_BATCH_188:   Batch 188: followed batch 187's NEXT_ACTION item (a) — spot-checked the 65 non-Play-game `HapticFeedback` call sites (antistress toys, calm toys, schedule/talk/lifeskills screens) for any doc comment falsely claiming to be gated by the Sensory Settings haptics toggle. Grepped case-insensitively for "sensory settings"/"hapticsEnabled"/"haptic toggle"/"haptic setting" across all of `lib/features/{antistress,calm,choices,feelings,firstthen,learn,lifeskills,schedule,sensorylab,talk,timer,universe,world}`, `lib/core`, and `lib/services` — the only two real hits were `tone_player.dart` (the actual implementation, correctly in-scope) and `toy_universe_screen.dart`, which on closer read doesn't even mention haptics (a grep context-line artifact from an adjacent match, confirmed via a follow-up haptic-only grep that returned nothing in that file). Lens exhausted, zero bugs — these 65 sites are correctly out-of-scope non-Play toys/screens per CLAUDE.md's SCOPE section and none of them misrepresent the toggle. Pivoted to item (b): fresh end-to-end reads of 8 arcade files not named in any of the last ~15 batches' candidate lists — `balloon_bounce_game.dart`, `bug_catch_game.dart`, `balloon_math_game.dart`, `count_pop_game.dart`, `shape_sort_chute_game.dart`, `star_path_game.dart`, `bubble_shooter_game.dart`, `shape_builder_game.dart` — against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria. 7 of 8 came back genuinely clean (escalating difficulty, distinct win/miss cues, aspect-correct hit-tests, colour-blind shape redundancy already in place). Found one real, concrete gameplay-loop gap in `letter_trace_game.dart`: its own intro text promises "follow the dotted path" to trace a letter, but `_touch` lit whichever unlit dot was globally nearest the touch point, with zero check that it was next in stroke order — a quick diagonal swipe across the screen could light dots scattered across multiple strokes out of sequence, skipping the actual letter shape entirely and defeating the fine-motor tracing purpose the whole game is built around (a child could "finish" a letter without ever tracing its real path). Fixed by replacing the nearest-unlit search with a single enforced `_nextDotIdx` target (first unlit dot in `_dots`, which is already built stroke-by-stroke in path order) — only that one dot can be lit on any given touch. Updated the Semantics overlay to expose only that same next dot (matching `star_path`'s guided next-star convention) instead of every remaining dot at once, and added a distinct highlighted ring in the painter so the enforced target is visibly discoverable rather than silently restrictive. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 4316f12.
PRIOR_BATCH_187:   Batch 187: followed batch 186's NEXT_ACTION — re-ran the "settings screen admits a known gap" lens repo-wide (grep for "not yet"/"coming soon"/"TODO"/"not implemented" across all of `lib/`). Every real hit was a false positive or an intentional, non-hedging pattern: `play_hub_screen.dart`'s "Coming soon" tile text and `toy_player_screen.dart`'s "coming soon" sheet are the deliberate, correct UI for any catalog entry with `toy.implemented == false` (a real, working, non-buggy fallback — not a documented hole like the haptics footer was; confirmed via `toy_registry.dart`/`toy_meta.dart` that zero toys currently have `implemented: false`, so this is live defensive code, not current dead weight); `learn_screen.dart`'s "coming soon" is just a comment bragging that nothing in that file is a stub. Lens fully exhausted, zero further gaps. Fell through to the NEXT_ACTION fallback and, instead of re-reading already-exhausted arcade files cold, verified the haptics toggle just added in batch 186 for actual completeness — grepped all 181 raw `HapticFeedback.*` call sites repo-wide and found the real, concrete gap: the toggle only gates `TonePlayer._triggerHaptic`, but all 78 Play games share one of three near-identical `_Shell`/`_GoalShell`/`_GameShell` base widgets (in `arcade_games.dart`/`goal_games.dart`/`mini_games.dart`) that each fire a direct `HapticFeedback.mediumImpact()`/`lightImpact()` on run-end completely bypassing `TonePlayer` — so turning the brand-new toggle off silently failed to mute the one haptic pulse every single Play game produces, undercutting the very fix batch 186 just shipped. Fixed by gating all three call sites on `TonePlayer.instance.hapticsEnabled` directly (same flag, now enforced at every haptic call site in the Play catalog, not just the `TonePlayer`-routed ones) and updated each site's explanatory comment to flag why the direct-call gate is needed. Fixed by adding `SensoryPreferences.hapticsEnabled` (default true, persisted as `haptics_enabled`, mirroring the existing `reduceMotion`/`musicEnabled` plumbing exactly), threading it through `TonePlayer.applyAudio`/`app.dart` the same way `musicEnabled` already flows, gating `_triggerHaptic` on it, and replacing the stale footer note with a real working `SwitchListTile` wired to a new `setHapticsEnabled` notifier method. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: a58ae44.

PRIOR_BATCH_182:   Batch 182: followed batch 181's NEXT_ACTION, reading all 19 named candidates fresh end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria: `skee_ball`, `air_hockey`, `mini_golf`, `basketball`, `soccer_kick`, `tic_tac_toe`, `block_blast`, `merge`, `ball_sort`, `jigsaw_four`, `balance_ball`, `calm_breaths`, `add_it_up`, `tone_match`, `echo_drums`, `drum_garden`, `xylophone_tap`, `quick_tap`, `pattern_weaver` — plus, once that list came back clean, a wider sweep of `counting_baskets`, `odd_one_out`, `bigger_number`, `weather_sort`, `steady_hand`, `rhythm_clap`. Found one genuine, concrete gap: `drum_garden_game.dart`'s own doc comment AND its in-game intro text both explicitly promise "tap freely to make music, then follow the growing tune" before the Simon round begins — but `onStart()` called `_startRound()` synchronously in the same frame, flipping phase 0 (free play) straight to phase 1 (showing) before a single frame ever rendered phase 0. The free-play feature the game's own first-interaction pitch leads with was dead code; a child's very first taps did nothing but wait in silence for the tune to start. Fixed by giving phase 0 a real ~2.6s window after Start (and after Play Again) where every pad tap genuinely plays its note and flashes — exactly what both the doc comment and the in-game banner claim — before `onTick` transitions into the first Simon tune. Every other candidate came back genuinely clean: all already have real escalating difficulty, distinct miss/win cues, aspect-correct hit-tests, and auto-clearing mistake flashes from the prior ~180 batches of fixes. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 79b4291.

PRIOR_BATCH_180:   Batch 180: followed batch 179's NEXT_ACTION, pivoting off the exhausted Semantics lens to re-audit the Phase-5 high-risk list for genuine gameplay-feel gaps. Read `RacingGame`, `SnakeGame`, `BowlingGame` (mini_games.dart), `GoalKeeperGame` (goal_games.dart), `StackGame` and `WhackGame` (arcade/*.dart) fresh end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria — all but Racing are already well-covered (streak/combo systems, escalating danger, aspect-correct hit-tests, distinct feedback cues, companion integration). Found one real, concrete gap in `RacingGame`: `_rivalLane` was populated once in `_startRace()` (`<int>[0, 2, 1]`) and never mutated again, so the 3 rival cars sat in one fixed lane for the entire race — a static backdrop gliding on parallel rails, while only the *player's* car actually slid/banked between lanes (`_lanePos` easing + `Transform.rotate` bank in the existing render code). This directly undercuts CLAUDE.md's "Racing like a real race" bar: a real race has rivals actually manoeuvring, not standing still sideways. Fixed by giving each rival a `_rivalLanePos` (eased visual lane, mirroring the player's own `_lanePos`) and a `_rivalLaneTimer` countdown (1.8-4.2s, randomized per rival) that periodically re-rolls `_rivalLane[i]` to a different lane; the render loop now interpolates each rival's position via the existing `_laneX3` helper and applies the same `Transform.rotate` bank-into-the-turn the player car uses, so an overtaking rival visibly steers instead of teleporting or standing still. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 3ea3f69.
PRIOR_BATCH_179:   Batch 179: followed batch 178's NEXT_ACTION, picking `star_path_game.dart` as the final confirmed-fit instance of the Semantics-coverage gap. PRIOR_BATCH_175:   Batch 175: followed batch 174's NEXT_ACTION, picking `shape_sort_chute_game.dart` as the next confirmed-fit instance of the Semantics-coverage gap. Wrapped the existing `GestureDetector`+`CustomPaint` in a `Stack` with the 3 overlays on top (same precedence as every prior Semantics fix — non-gesture `SizedBox.expand` children don't block the drag/tap hit-test below). PRIOR_BATCH_165:   Batch 165: followed batch 164's NEXT_ACTION, reading the 7 named not-recently-checked candidates fresh end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria: `bubble_shooter_game.dart` (escalating ceiling-drop pressure, distinct pop/launch/drop cues, aspect-safe hit tests — no gap), `air_hockey_game.dart` (aspect-correct puck/paddle collision already fixed, distinct goal-scored/match-lost cues, reactive-then-attacking AI — no gap), `mini_golf_game.dart` (two-obstacle escalation from hole 6, tightening sink-speed window on the back nine, aspect-correct cup/wall collisions — no gap), `calm_choices_game.dart` (shuffled-bag no-repeat scenario draw, distinct life-ending cue, auto-clearing wrong flash — no gap), `bigger_number_game.dart` (gentle widening number-range ramp, auto-clearing wrong flash — no gap), `weather_sort_game.dart` (shuffled-bag draw, distinct terminal cue, auto-clearing flash — no gap), `calm_choices`/`bigger_number`/`weather_sort`'s fixed 3-option layouts are intentionally bounded (3 real weather types / fixed biggest-smallest comparison), not an instance of the challenge-curve bug class — no gap in any of these six. Found one real, concrete gap in `color_mixer_game.dart`: its "Your bowl" swatch was wrapped in a `TweenAnimationBuilder<Color?>` with `ColorTween(begin: mix, end: mix)` — both ends of the tween were the SAME freshly-computed `mix` value on every rebuild (an interpolation between two identical colours is a no-op), so the smooth colour-blend polish this widget was clearly built for never actually ran; every pour just snapped the bowl to its new colour instantly with zero visible mixing/swirl, in the one game whose entire premise is watching colours blend. Fixed by swapping to a plain `AnimatedContainer`, which genuinely animates its `color` property by diffing against the actual previous widget across rebuilds (the same implicit-animation pattern `_CalmOption`/`bigger_number`'s number tiles already use elsewhere in the catalog). Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 2c33a16.
PRIOR_BATCH_164:   Batch 164: followed batch 163's NEXT_ACTION, finishing the "distinct wrong-tap colour/flash" lens against the four named candidates. `brick_break_game.dart` is not an instance of this bug class — it's a lives/paddle game, not a tap-the-right-thing game, and a lost ball already gets its own distinct 'Ball lost · N left' banner + crash sound cue (vs. 'Cracked!'/'Nice hit!' + brick/success cues on a real hit) — no gap. `basketball_game.dart` is also not an instance — misses already get a distinct 'Miss — try again' banner with no net-wobble/particle burst, clearly different from a swish's confetti + success cue — no gap. `sky_hop_game.dart` is not applicable either — it's continuous flap-to-dodge gameplay with no tap-the-right-thing target, so there's no "wrong tap" to distinguish from a "right tap". Found one real, concrete gap in `piano_tiles_game.dart` itself — the Phase-5 benchmark game: unlike every other tap-the-right-thing game in the catalog, a wrong-column tap didn't even reuse the correct-tap highlight — it rendered NO flash at all on the tapped column, going straight to the game-over overlay with zero visual feedback on the actual mistake, an even starker version of the bug class than `echo_game`/`catch_beat`'s "same colour as correct". Fixed by adding a `_flashWrong` flag, set alongside `_flashCol`/`_flashT` on the fatal miss (mirroring `echo_game`'s pattern of setting the flash in the same beat as the game-over status so it still paints under the dark overlay), so the tapped column renders the catalog-wide red tint (`0xFFE23B3B`) instead of the purple hit-flash or nothing at all. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 26f6621.
PRIOR_BATCH_163:   Batch 163: followed batch 162's NEXT_ACTION "distinct wrong-tap colour/flash" lens against the named candidates. `whack_game.dart` already distinct (bomb renders its own 💣 emoji before the tap and the hit-splat effect is only ever added on the correct-mole path, never on a bomb hit — no gap). `quick_tap_game.dart` already distinct (tapping on red flips the whole screen to an orange "Too soon!" state, nothing like the green success flash — no gap). Found one real, concrete gap in `catch_beat_game.dart`: unlike `echo_game` (batch 162) and the rest of the catalog's tap-the-right-thing games, tapping a lane with NO catchable orb in the hit window still flashed that lane in its own assigned colour (`colors[lane]`) — the exact same visual as a real catch — so a wrong/empty tap gave a child zero distinct signal beyond the combo silently resetting later. Fixed by tracking whether the flash corresponds to a hit or a miss (`_laneFlashHit`) and rendering misses in the catalog-wide red tint (`0xFFE23B3B`) instead of the lane's colour, matching the same red-flash convention established by `echo_game`/`shadow_match`/`spot_difference`/`pattern_weaver`/`weather_sort`. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 5008373.
PRIOR_BATCH_162:   Batch 162: followed batch 161's NEXT_ACTION layout-robustness lens — grepped every arcade/mini/goal game for `mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.spaceEvenly` `Row(` option layouts (`bigger_number`, `weather_sort`, `color_mixer`) and confirmed all three use a genuinely FIXED option count (3 numbers / 3 weather bins / fixed drop colours, never escalating), so none can ever overflow — lens came up clean, `feelings_match` (batch 161) remains the only real instance. Fell back to re-reading games not yet explicitly re-checked in the last ~15 batches: `pinball_game.dart` and `space_dodge_game.dart` (both Phase-5 high-risk games) and `stack_game.dart`, `spot_difference_game.dart`, `pattern_weaver_game.dart` were all read fresh end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria — all five already solid (extra-ball mechanic, two-finger flipper holds, aspect-correct bumper/meteor hit-tests, wave/dash/mastery-reset escalation, auto-clearing wrong-flashes) — no gap in any. Found one real, concrete gap in `echo_game.dart`: unlike every other tap-the-right-thing game in the catalog (`shadow_match`/`spot_difference`/`pattern_weaver`/`weather_sort` all flash the touched tile/pad red on a miss), a wrong tap in Echo lit the pad with the EXACT SAME colour/glow as a correct tap (`_tapFlash` was shared between both paths) — the only signal a child got that they'd broken the sequence was the full-screen game-over card a moment later, with zero distinct feedback on the pad itself. Fixed by adding a dedicated `_wrongPad` flag, set only on the wrong-answer path, that renders the touched pad with the catalog-wide red tint (`0xFFE23B3B`) instead of its own colour, reset in both `_nextRound()` and `_reset()`. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 761ffcd. PRIOR_BATCH_161:   Batch 161: followed batch 160's NEXT_ACTION — applied the "challenge curve" lens to the named round-based list: `merge` (tile-value/milestone escalation is intrinsic to 2048-style merging — not an instance), `odd_one_out` (already escalates grid `_cols` 2→5 and subtle-color delta with score — no gap), `shadow_match` (already escalates `_optionCount` 4→6 with score — no gap), `weather_sort`/`kindness_match` (both already draw from a shuffled anti-repeat bag across a fixed 18/10-item content set — finite vocabulary-teaching games where the "escalation" is realistically just full content coverage, not an instance of the bug class), `add_it_up`/`bigger_number`/`balloon_math` (all three already carry real escalating-range/tile-count/subtraction-unlock formulas from earlier batches — no gap). Fixed by adding a shuffled-bag prompt draw (no immediate repeats until all 8 feelings are seen) and an escalating option count (`3 + score~/3`, clamped to the full 8-feeling roster) so the final rounds are a genuinely harder face-scan than round 1 — same "found by applying the challenge-curve lens" pattern as batch 160's `tap_order` fix. Read `xylophone_tap`/`quick_tap`/`beat_builder`/`count_pop` end-to-end — all four already carry real fixes from earlier commits (wrong-bar flash, stale-best rebuild fix, win-on-full-groove, difficulty-floor + legible counter + aspect-correct hit-test respectively) — no gap in any. Tried a fresh painter-unused-constructor-field static scan (precedent: batch 152's bug_catch fix) across all 72 `CustomPainter` subclasses catalog-wide — the only two hits (`jigsaw_four_game.dart`'s `hh`/`hy`) were a regex false-positive (local vars inside a nested `_scene()` helper, not unused fields); confirmed by reading the file that `_JigsawPainter` has no actual gap. Cross-checked the reduce-motion lens again (every file using `_Shard(` particle effects already calls `MediaQuery.disableAnimationsOf`/`_reduceMotion` — fully exhausted, matches commit 7d8656e). With all of these lenses coming up clean, read `tap_order_game.dart` fresh against the "challenge curve" criterion specifically (not yet explicitly checked under this lens) and found a real, concrete gap: unlike every other round-based game in the catalog, each of its rounds is the exact same flat 1-25 shuffle forever with zero escalation — no difficulty curve, no progression signal at all beyond a round counter. Fixed by tracking real per-round completion time (DateTime-based, no ticker needed since the widget has no `onTick`) and surfacing an honest session personal-best on the round-complete banner, matching the established "surface a real stat on both win/loss, not a gimmick" pattern (`catch_beat`'s best-combo, `quick_tap`'s reaction-time rating) — gives a genuine, non-punishing replay goal (beat your own speed) instead of pure repetition. Checked the five named candidates: `slide_puzzle_game.dart` (always a fixed 3x3 board, no per-level resizing — not an instance), `block_blast_game.dart` (fixed 8x8 board — not an instance), `jigsaw_four_game.dart` (explicit `[cols,rows]` pairs table `[2,2]/[3,2]/[3,3]` always an exact rectangle by construction — not an instance), `ball_sort_game.dart`/`shape_builder_game.dart` (neither uses a `GridView`/fixed-column layout at all — not instances). Broadened the sweep catalog-wide by grepping for a column-count ternary/threshold pattern (`length <=` driving a `GridView.count`) and found the exact same bug class in `memory_flip_game.dart` (a *different* memory-match game from batch 154's `memory_pairs_deluxe` fix, sharing the same flaw independently): `final cols = _cards.length <= 12 ? 3 : 4;` combined with the escalating pair formula `(5 + level).clamp(4, 10)` produced card counts of 12/14/16/18/20 across its 5 levels — 14 cards (level 2, 7 pairs) and 18 cards (level 4, 9 pairs) both land in the `cols = 4` branch, and neither 14 nor 18 divides evenly by 4, leaving a dangling half-empty last row exactly like the `memory_deluxe` bug (12/16/20 happened to divide evenly, which is why this was never visually obvious at every level). Fixed with the same two-part approach as batch 154: (1) replaced the linear pair formula with an explicit `_pairsSchedule = [6, 8, 9, 10, 10]` chosen so every resulting card count (12/16/18/20/20) has a column count that divides it evenly, and (2) replaced the 2-branch cols ternary with an explicit per-count `_cols` mapping (12→3, 16→4, 18→6, 20→4) so the board is always a clean rectangle; also widened the card-face font-size scaling (`cols<=3?40:cols==4?30:24`) to stay legible at the new 6-column case. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 2182ee9. Re-ran the mention-count script fresh; lowest-count candidates were `star_tap` (26) and `fruit_catch` (27) — read both `StarTapGame`/`_StarTapGameState` and `FruitCatchGame`/`_FruitCatchGameState` in `mini_games.dart` end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria: both already escalate (star fade-time and decoy-chance scale with score; fruit fall-speed and spawn-rate scale with score), both give honest non-silent feedback on every miss (star-fade, decoy-tap, empty-cell-tap, fruit-falls-past, bomb-catch all flash+sound), both already have reduce-motion handling where relevant — no gap in either. Tried NEXT_ACTION candidate (A) — wrote a one-off script scanning every `CustomPainter` subclass across `lib/features/play/toys/**/*.dart` for a constructor field never referenced inside its own `paint()` body (the "computed-but-never-rendered state" bug class from batch 118's goal_keeper fix). Found exactly one real hit: `bug_catch_game.dart`'s `_BugPainter` received a `targetColor` field that was never used anywhere in `paint()` — every bug relied solely on its own fill hue (`colors[b.colorIndex]`) to signal a match, with zero non-colour cue, meaning a colour-blind child distinguishing the four palette hues (red/yellow/green/blue) by hue alone had no fallback tell for which bug was the current target. Fixed by drawing a pulsing white ring (`r * (1.35 + pulse * 0.18)`, opacity driven by `math.sin(b.phase * 0.6)`, reusing the bug's own existing `phase` field so no new per-bug state was needed) around every bug whose `colorIndex == targetColor`, giving a shape/glow-based tell independent of hue. Candidate (B) (generic shared-shell winText audit) was also checked: every `_Shell`-style arcade game missing a bespoke `winText` (`ball_sort`, `block_blast`, `brick_break`, `bubble_shooter`, `piano_tiles`, `pinball`, `stack`, `tap_order`) was confirmed to be an endless/survival game with only `GameStatus.over`, never `GameStatus.won` — so the default `winText` is genuinely unreachable dead code, not a real gap (same conclusion batch 31 already reached; don't re-litigate without a new trigger). The painter-unused-field scan is a promising NEW lens — this batch only found the one hit catalog-wide (scanned all 46+ arcade `CustomPainter` classes plus `mini_games.dart`/`goal_games.dart`), so it's effectively exhausted too, but worth a quick re-run if new painters are added later. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: ca59003.
PRIOR_BATCH_151:   Batch 151: followed batch 150's NEXT_ACTION. Re-ran the mention-count script fresh; lowest-count candidates (`fishing`/`memory_pairs_deluxe` 16-17, `ball_sort`/`tic_tac_toe` 18) have all been re-checked repeatedly in the last several cycles with no new gap, so pursued the NEXT_ACTION's suggested fresh lens instead: re-check drag-and-drop games for whether a piece/ball left mid-drag by an interrupted gesture (`onPanCancel`, not just `onPanEnd`) is actually handled, beyond the three names (`jigsaw_four`, `maze_marble`, `mirror_draw`) the prior batch flagged. Confirmed `jigsaw_four`/`maze_marble` already carry the fix, and `mirror_draw` isn't an instance of the bug class at all (it only ever lights dots permanently on touch — there's no draggable item/index to leave orphaned). Broadened the sweep catalog-wide: grepped every `onPanStart`/`onPanUpdate`/`onPanEnd` handler for files missing `onPanCancel`, then individually read each candidate for whether it actually holds a "dragging item index" or a gating bool that would get stuck. Most (`air_hockey`, `brick_break`, `bubble_wrap`, `letter_trace`, `trace_game`, `star_path`, `space_dodge`, `shape_sort_chute`, `maze_run`, `goal_games`'s `_GoalKeeperGame`, `mini_games`'s bowling/snake/racing) just map the finger position directly every frame with no persisted drag-state to orphan — not instances. Found two real, concrete instances of the exact bug class: (1) `counting_baskets_game.dart`'s `_dragging` (a fruit index) stayed set forever on a cancelled pan, stranding the fruit wherever it was last dragged to with the basket-mouth check never run on it; (2) `steady_hand_game.dart`'s `_holding` bool stayed stuck `true` forever on a cancelled pan — worse than counting_baskets, because the dot would then render as "held" with no finger on it, and the very next unrelated touch anywhere on screen would immediately start dragging the dot (bypassing the "grab the dot" requirement entirely) since `onPanUpdate` only gates on `_holding`. Fixed both by adding `onPanCancel` that runs the exact same resolution `onPanEnd` already does (basket check for counting_baskets; `_holding = false` for steady_hand), mirroring the established `jigsaw_four`/`maze_marble` pattern. This closes the interrupted-drag-recovery lens catalog-wide — every `onPan*` drag handler with a persisted drag-state has now been individually checked (fixed or confirmed not-an-instance). Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 163160c.

Found one real, concrete instance of the exact bug class in `block_blast_game.dart`: `_place(r, c)` returned silently (`if (p == null || !_fits(p, r, c)) return;`) whenever the selected piece didn't fit at the tapped cell (off the grid edge or overlapping a filled cell) — a child who picked a piece and tapped a cell it couldn't legally occupy got total silence, indistinguishable from a tap that never registered, exactly the class fixed in `slide_puzzle` (batch 149) and `color_mixer` (batch 145) applied to a third game. Fixed by tracking the attempted piece's cells in a new `_invalidCells` list, flashing them red for 300ms (same `Future.delayed` + token-guard auto-clear pattern used catalog-wide) and playing `SoundCue.gentleRetry`, so an illegal placement now gives honest, immediate feedback instead of silence. PRIOR_BATCH_147:   Batch 147: re-ran the mention-count script fresh (regex-escaped base-name count across this file). Read all four end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria. `fishing_game.dart` (honest shrinking bite window as score climbs, golden-fish bonus, already-fixed catchable-depth band per its own prior comment — no gap), `ball_sort_game.dart` (bounded-BFS solvability check on every deal so a shuffle can never produce an unsolvable board, colour count escalates with level — no gap), and `beat_builder_game.dart` (sequencer fills-the-grid win already wired to a real `GameStatus.won` per its own prior fix comment — no gap) were all already solid. Found one real, concrete bug in `rhythm_clap_game.dart`: the playhead-miss handler in `onTick()` marked a missed beat marker with the SAME `_hit[i] = true` flag used for a genuine on-beat tap, and the painter colored every `_hit[i]` marker gold regardless of which path set it — so a beat the child missed (losing a life) rendered with the exact same gold "success" colour as one they actually clapped on time, directly contradicting the lives counter and undermining the only feedback the marker itself gives. Fixed by splitting the single `_hit` flag into two: `_hit` (genuine on-beat tap, stays gold) and a new `_missed` array (resolved-as-a-miss, renders dim `Colors.white24` instead), both reset together in `_newBar()`, with the resolution checks in `onTick()`/`_clap()` now guarding on `!_hit[i] && !_missed[i]` so a marker can only ever be claimed once by whichever path reaches it first. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 7f73774.

Both already solid: `feelings_match` has an honest auto-clearing wrong-flash, distinct game-over cue, no gap. `soccer_kick`'s launch-gate/aim-preview sign handling looked suspicious at first read (gating on raw `dy` sign while the trajectory and preview force an unconditional `-abs(dy)`) but tracing it fully confirmed this is a deliberate, already-documented fix from an earlier batch (the in-file comment explicitly explains why both must agree) — left untouched to avoid re-litigating a prior fix. Broadened the sweep to the rest of the batch-145 queue (`skee_ball`, `target_toss`, `add_it_up`, `maze_marble`, `firefly_count`, `maze_run`) — `target_toss` (ring speed escalates with score), `add_it_up` (tile count/value range escalates, wrong-flash clears on next tap not stuck), `maze_marble` (board count/gap width escalates, aspect-correct physics), `firefly_count` (count range escalates, auto-clearing flash, Semantics overlays), and `maze_run` (maze size escalates, audible blocked-swipe cue) were all already solid — no gap in any. Found one real, concrete gap in `skee_ball_game.dart`: unlike every other game reaching a 100/10-style target (`target_toss` speeds its target up, `soccer_kick` sharpens the keeper's read as score climbs), skee_ball's four scoring bands (`_bandUpperY`) were `static const` and never moved for the entire run — landing a 50 on throw 1 required the exact same drag precision as landing a 50 on throw 20, so a 100-point run had zero difficulty curve despite being one of the longest games in the catalog. Fixed by adding `_bandUpperYHard` (a fully-escalated, narrower band set) and a `_liveBandUpperY` getter that lerps from the easy opening bands to the hard late-game bands as `_score` climbs toward `_target`, so precision genuinely has to improve over the course of a run. Threaded the live bands through to `_resolve()` (real scoring) and the painter (now takes `bandUpperY` as a parameter instead of reading the static field directly) so the decorative rings can never drift out of sync with what actually scores — same shared-source-of-truth discipline the file's own prior comments already established for the static bands. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: e9ba440.
PRIOR_BATCH_145:   Batch 145: followed batch 144's NEXT_ACTION — continued the regular arcade mention-count sweep, re-ranking by lowest-count/not-yet-this-cycle: `echo_drums_game.dart`, `count_pop_game.dart`, `balance_ball_game.dart`, `hoop_toss_game.dart`, `steady_hand_game.dart`, `shape_sort_chute_game.dart` were all fully read against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria — all six already solid (several carry their own prior aspect-ratio/onPanCancel/stuck-flash fix comments from earlier batches, no new gap in any). Found one real, concrete gap in `color_mixer_game.dart`: unlike the stuck-wrong-flash bug class fixed catalog-wide elsewhere, this game had NO wrong-answer feedback at all — `_addDrop()` only ever reacted when the mixed distance to the target dropped below the match threshold; a child blind-guessing primary colours got total silence on every drop that didn't land the exact match, so diluting the bowl with the wrong colour for 10+ drops in a row gave zero signal anything was off (worse than a stuck flash — there was never ANY flash). Fixed by comparing each drop's new mix-distance-to-target against the distance just before that drop and surfacing an honest `'Getting warmer!'` banner when it improved or `'Getting colder — try Empty the bowl'` with a `SoundCue.gentleRetry` cue when it got worse, so the core mixing-by-feel loop this game exists to teach now actually teaches instead of leaving a guessing child with no signal at all. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: ff1b1c4.
PRIOR_BATCH_144:   Batch 144: followed batch 143's NEXT_ACTION — resumed the regular arcade mention-count sweep, picking the queued names not yet re-read this cycle: `piano_song_game.dart`, `xylophone_tap_game.dart`, `dot_to_dot_game.dart`, `tone_match_game.dart`. Read all four end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's full criteria. `dot_to_dot_game.dart` (already aspect-ratio-safe per batch 136's own check, has Semantics overlays for screen-reader dot discovery, auto-clearing `_wrongDot` flash via ToyTicker — no gap) and `tone_match_game.dart` (honest listen-then-match-by-ear memory game, fair reveal/hide timing, no silent misses — no gap) were both already solid. Found one real, concrete UX gap shared by `piano_song_game.dart` and `xylophone_tap_game.dart`: in both, a wrong tap only ever changed the banner text at the TOP of the screen — the actual key/bar the child's finger touched gave zero visual sign it was the wrong one, unlike the catalog-wide stuck/brief-wrong-flash pattern already used everywhere else a wrong answer exists (`dot_to_dot`'s `_wrongDot` ring, `odd_one_out`/`shadow_match`/`spot_difference`'s red flash, etc). For a child following "the glowing key," a banner at the top is easy to miss entirely while looking at the keyboard/bars — the feedback needs to be ON the thing they touched. Fixed both: added a `_wrongKey`/`_wrongBar` index that's set on a wrong tap, renders a brief red flash (border + fill tint) on exactly that key/bar, and auto-clears via the same `Future.delayed(300ms)` + `mounted && _wrongX == x` guard pattern used catalog-wide, so a later miss on a different key/bar can't be stomped by a stale timer. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 53ec418.
PRIOR_BATCH_143:   Batch 143: followed batch 142's NEXT_ACTION — did the dedicated catalog-wide grep sweep for the remaining aspect-ratio hit-test bug class (`.abs() < 0.0` / fixed-tolerance distance comparisons against independently w/h-normalized coordinates) across every `arcade/*.dart` tap/touch/drag handler not yet individually checked: `basketball_game.dart` (rim-knob collision already explicitly aspect-corrected per its own prior fix comment — scales the y-delta by `_aspect` before computing the collision distance — no gap), `sky_hop_game.dart` (coin/pipe collision checks are plain axis-aligned AABB tests where x is genuinely width-domain and y genuinely height-domain, not a cross-axis distance formula — not an instance of the bug), and `skee_ball_game.dart` (`_targetY`/`_ballY` comparison is an internal single-axis roll-settle check, no touch coordinates involved — not an instance). Found one real, concrete instance of the SAME bug class in `mirror_draw_game.dart`: `_touch()` compared `(d.x - nx).abs() < 0.06` and `(d.y - ny).abs() < 0.06` independently against width- and height-normalized coordinates, while the painter draws every dot as a true isotropic circle (fixed 7-12px radius, same on both axes regardless of canvas size). On a portrait phone (h > w) the vertical tracing tolerance in pixels was noticeably wider than the horizontal one, desyncing the forgiving-tap zone from the visible dot on one axis vs. the other. Fixed by tracking `_aspect = h/w` on every touch (same established pattern as `letter_trace`/`star_path`/`bug_catch`) and scaling the y-delta by it before comparing, so both axes compare in width-normalized pixel-equivalent units. With this sweep, every arcade file's touch/drag hit-test has now been individually checked for this bug class — it is CLOSED catalog-wide (confirmed either safe or fixed in all of: `bug_catch`, `shape_builder`, `counting_baskets`, `letter_trace`, `star_path`, `balloon_math`, `balloon_bounce`, `dot_to_dot`, `jigsaw_four`, `basketball`, `sky_hop`, `skee_ball`, `mirror_draw`). Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 5a5f971.

Found one real, concrete bug in `star_path_game.dart`: the SAME aspect-ratio hit-test bug class fixed in `bug_catch`/`shape_builder`/`counting_baskets`/`letter_trace` — `_touch()` compared `(s[0]-nx).abs() < 0.07` and `(s[1]-ny).abs() < 0.07` independently against width- and height-normalized coordinates, while the star is drawn as a true isotropic circle (same pixel radius on both axes). On a portrait phone (h > w) the vertical catch tolerance in pixels was wider than the horizontal one, desyncing the hit box from the visible star — a child could tap well above/below a star and still catch it, or tap visually on-target horizontally-close stars and miss. Fixed by tracking `_aspect = h/w` on every touch (same pattern as `letter_trace`) and scaling the y-delta by it before comparing, so both axes compare in width-normalized pixel-equivalent units. PRIOR_BATCH_141:   Batch 141: followed batch 140's NEXT_ACTION — ran the dedicated catalog-wide grep sweep for the stuck-wrong-flash bug class (`_wrong\w*\s*=` / `_wrongFlash` / `_wrongIndex` / `_wrongDot`) across every `arcade/*.dart` file to find any remaining un-checked instance, rather than re-reading the next lowest-mention queue names first. The sweep surfaced two NOT-yet-checked matches: `pattern_weaver_game.dart` and `shadow_match_game.dart` (plus `tap_order_game.dart`, already previously fixed). Read `pattern_weaver_game.dart`'s flash handling end-to-end: `_wrongFlash`/`_wrongT` are already auto-cleared by a per-tick countdown (`_wrongT -= dt; if (_wrongT <= 0) _wrongFlash = -1;`) — already safe, no gap. Found one real, concrete bug in `shadow_match_game.dart`: the SAME stuck-wrong-flash bug class fixed in 8 prior games/shells (`weather_sort`/`bigger_number`/`calm_choices`/`kindness_match`/`feelings_match`/`odd_one_out`/`_ChoiceGoalGame`/`spot_difference`) — `_wrongFlash` (the red-ring highlight on an incorrectly tapped shadow) was set on every wrong tap in `_pick()` but only ever reset to `-1` inside `_newRound()`, which only runs after the child eventually taps the correct shadow. Then fully re-read `tone_match`, `star_path`, `xylophone_tap`, `counting_baskets`, `piano_song`, `count_pop`, `add_it_up` end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's criteria — all seven were already solid (tone_match/star_path's hide-timers and no-repeat shuffles, xylophone_tap/piano_song's shuffled song order and forgiving wrong-note handling with no stuck state, counting_baskets/count_pop's genuinely escalating target-count ramps, add_it_up's already-correct `_wrong = -1` reset at the top of every `_tap()` call so a miss clears on the very next interaction). Found one real, concrete instance of the exact SAME stuck-flash bug class in `bigger_number_game.dart`: `_wrong` (the red highlight on an incorrectly tapped number tile) was set on a miss but only ever cleared by `_newRound()` — which only runs after the child correctly solves the CURRENT round, not on the next tap and not on any timer. A child who missed, then paused (or missed again without yet solving the round), saw the tile stuck red indefinitely — unlike every other miss-flash in the catalog's brief ~350-500ms cue. Fixed with the same `Future.delayed(500ms)` auto-clear pattern used by `weather_sort`/`shape_builder`, gated on `mounted && _wrong == i` so a later miss on a different tile can't be stomped by a stale timer. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 1e2b4e2.
PRIOR_BATCH_132:   Batch 132: followed batch 131's NEXT_ACTION — picked `weather_sort_game.dart` from the queued candidate list for a full honest replay pass (first 3 seconds, difficulty curve, replayability, visual polish, sensory appropriateness). The bag-shuffle no-repeat draw, lives system, distinct win/loss cues, and milestone-free-but-clear banner text were all already solid. Found one real, concrete feel gap: `_wrongFlash` (the red highlight on an incorrectly tapped bin) was set on a miss but NEVER cleared except by the next correct answer's `_newItem()` call — unlike every other sort/match game in the catalog (`shape_builder_game.dart` clears it via `Future.delayed(350ms)`, `sorting_train_game.dart` clears it via a per-tick countdown), which treat the wrong-flash as a momentary "that's wrong" cue, not a sticky state. A child missing twice in a row (or missing then pausing) would see a bin stuck red indefinitely instead of a brief, honest mistake flash. Fixed by adding the same `Future.delayed(350ms)` auto-clear pattern used by `shape_builder`, gated on `mounted && _wrongFlash == bin` so a later miss on a different bin can't be stomped by a stale timer. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 0558415.
PRIOR_BATCH_131:   Batch 131: followed batch 130's NEXT_ACTION — did the targeted grep sweep for any remaining `onPanEnd`/`onTapDown`/gesture handler with an early `return;` guard and no sound/banner, across `pinball`, `tic_tac_toe`, `balance_ball`, `soccer_kick`, `skee_ball`, `bowling` (the files not yet covered by the silent-blocked-gesture lens). `pinball` and `bowling` have no matching gesture handlers in this shape (not applicable). `tic_tac_toe`'s `onTapDown` already routes through `_tap(i)` which no-ops silently on an already-filled cell — judged acceptable since the cell visually shows an X/O already, so there's no ambiguity to resolve (not a gap). `balance_ball`'s `onPanEnd`/`onPanCancel` just stop a continuous tilt drag — no discrete "blocked" outcome exists there. `soccer_kick`'s `onPanEnd` always calls `_launch()` unconditionally once dragging — no blocked branch. Found one real, concrete gap in `skee_ball_game.dart`: `onPanEnd` called `_launch()` only `if (_power > 0.05)`, and the `else` branch was completely silent — a child releasing a weak flick got no sound, no banner, nothing — the same silent-blocked-gesture bug class now found in a 5th game. Fixed by playing `SoundCue.gentleRetry` on that branch (matching maze_run/basketball/merge) and resetting `_power` via `setState` so the "Drag up to roll" idle label reappears immediately instead of waiting for the next tick. The silent-blocked-gesture lens is now genuinely closed catalog-wide (checked every file with this gesture shape). Then fixed the `maze_run` swipe-into-a-closed-wall gap flagged by batch 127: a swipe that didn't move the dot at all (blocked immediately by a wall) was completely silent — no sound, no feedback — so a child couldn't tell "that wall is closed" from "my swipe wasn't registered." Also re-read `shape_sort_chute_game.dart` (queued candidate) end-to-end — already excellent (escalating fall speed via `_fall = 0.26 + _score * 0.012`, distinct win/loss/wrong-hole cues, correct submit-on-score pattern) — no gap. The final, longest phrase/tune a child ever completes (the one that actually wins the game) was silently excluded from their best score, so the win screen showed a stale best from one round earlier instead of the run that just won. Fixed both by adding the same fire-and-forget-then-setState submit call used by every other call site in each file. PRIOR_BATCH_125:   Batch 125: followed batch 124's NEXT_ACTION — fully re-read the 7 queued games (`pinball`, `tic_tac_toe`, `air_hockey`, `mini_golf`, `basketball`, `hoop_toss`, `soccer_kick`, `balance_ball`) end-to-end against CLAUDE.md section 3's criteria. Six were already excellent (pinball's extra-ball milestone/combo system/two-finger holds, air_hockey/mini_golf/basketball's aspect-ratio-correct collisions and eased difficulty ramps, tic_tac_toe's beatable-but-not-trivial AI, hoop_toss's and balance_ball's tightening timing/tolerance curves — no gaps). Found one real, concrete bug in `soccer_kick_game.dart`: its real kick physics (`_bvy = -dy.abs() * k - 0.2`) always launches the ball *upward* toward the goal regardless of which way the vertical pull actually points, but the painter's dotted aim-preview arrow independently recomputed a raw, unconditional `(ball - aim)` direction instead of sharing that formula — so for the only drag that actually fires a shot (finger held above the ball, the required `dy > 0` gate), the preview arrow pointed straight *down*, away from the goal, while the ball was about to fly up. The single most important piece of aiming feedback in the game showed the child the exact opposite of what was about to happen. Fixed by extracting a shared `_launchVel(dx, dy)` used by the real kick, and mirroring the same unconditional `-dy.abs()` in the painter's preview direction so the two can never disagree again (matches the `_launchVel`/`launch` shared-computation pattern already used correctly in `basketball_game.dart` and `mini_golf_game.dart`). Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 63ff1a9.

First fully re-read `quick_tap_game.dart` end-to-end (candidate 2) — confirmed already correct: both `.then()` call sites properly setState `_best`, the random 0.9–3.1s wait window avoids a learnable fixed cadence, and the reaction-speed scoring curve (`max(5, 120 - ms/10)`) rewards faster taps smoothly. No gap found. Then fixed candidate 1, `skee_ball_game.dart`'s decorative scoring rings (flagged unfixed since batch 121): the rings used an independent, hand-tuned `[centerY, radius, pts]` list that had drifted out of alignment with the real thresholds in `_resolve()` (e.g. the 50-pt ring's visual extent reached y=0.23 while the real 50-pt band ends at y=0.2, overlapping into the 30-pt band), and used a width-based (`w *`) radius to position a circle on the y-axis, so alignment silently depended on the canvas being square. Fixed by extracting the real thresholds into shared `_bandUpperY`/`_bandPts` constants used by both `_resolve()` and the painter, and replacing the circles with ovals drawn to exactly span each band's real vertical extent — the visual scoring zone can now never drift out of sync with what actually scores again. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: e5a8c03.
PRIOR_BATCH_123:   Batch 123: followed batch 122's NEXT_ACTION — grepped every `GameScores.instance.submit(` call site catalog-wide (91 matches across 69 files, not just the already-known `.then((b) { ... _best = b; })` textual pattern) to catch any differently-phrased variant of the same stale-`_best` bug class. Found + fixed one real instance in `echo_game.dart`: its win-path (`_seq.length >= _target`) called `GameScores.instance.submit(_id, _seq.length)` completely fire-and-forget with no `.then()` at all, unlike its own other two call sites in the same file (loss path + round-advance path) which both correctly chain `.then((b) { if (mounted) setState(() => _best = b); })`. Since the win overlay's `_Shell` reads `best: _best`, a player reaching the win target for the first time (or beating their prior best on a winning run) saw the win screen show their *old* best score instead of the one they just set. Fixed by adding the same `.then()` + `setState` pattern used by every other call site. Also re-verified `bubble_shooter_game.dart` end-to-end per the NEXT_ACTION (both submit call sites, difficulty ramp via `_dropCeiling`, flood-fill matching, launch/pop audio cues, loss condition) — confirmed fully correct, no further gap. Two other no-`.then()` call sites found in the same grep (`bubble_wrap_game.dart`, `beat_builder_game.dart`) were investigated and confirmed NOT bugs: both mutate `_best` synchronously inline (not inside the async callback) before their own enclosing `setState()` call/block resolves, so the rebuild already picks up the correct value — only `echo_game.dart`'s was a genuine stale-async-mutation bug. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: d87f735.
PRIOR_BATCH_122:   Batch 122: followed batch 121's NEXT_ACTION — fully re-read 24 non-high-risk games queued for a holistic feel pass: `color_mixer_game.dart`, `merge_game.dart`, `weather_sort_game.dart`, `tone_match_game.dart`, `tap_order_game.dart`, `star_path_game.dart`, `spot_difference_game.dart`, `shape_builder_game.dart`, `xylophone_tap_game.dart`, `counting_baskets_game.dart`, `piano_song_game.dart`, `mirror_draw_game.dart`, `count_pop_game.dart`, `calm_choices_game.dart`, `calm_breaths_game.dart`, `bigger_number_game.dart`, `add_it_up_game.dart`, `kindness_match_game.dart`, `feelings_match_game.dart`, `shadow_match_game.dart`, `block_blast_game.dart`, `balloon_bounce_game.dart`, `beat_builder_game.dart`. Nearly all were already excellent (prior batches had clearly already swept this group — difficulty ramps, bag-shuffle no-repeat draws, distinct terminal-miss cues, legible text contrast, and result-flash feedback were already in place everywhere). Found one real, different-class bug via a targeted grep for the exact `GameScores.instance.submit(...).then((b) { ... _best = b; })` pattern across every arcade file: `block_blast_game.dart`, `bubble_shooter_game.dart` and `quick_tap_game.dart` all updated the `_best` field inside that async callback WITHOUT wrapping it in `setState()` (unlike every other game's identical pattern, which does `setState(() => _best = b)`). Since `GameScores.submit()`'s future resolves after the current frame's own synchronous `setState(() {})` has already run, the mutation was invisible to Flutter's rebuild scheduler — a child beating their own best score mid-run saw the stat silently stay stale on screen until some unrelated later interaction happened to trigger a repaint. Fixed all three call sites to match the correct pattern used everywhere else. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 5594611.

Found one real, concrete feel gap via data-flow tracing: `_GoalKeeperGameState` computes `_lastSaved` and threads it all the way into `_KeeperPainter` as a constructor field, but `paint()` never actually reads it — a save and a goal looked visually IDENTICAL on the goalmouth itself (only the text banner + particle colour differed), so the single most important moment in the game (did I make the save?) had no dedicated visual payoff. Fixed: added a `phaseT` field to the painter (the existing 0.85s post-shot pause timer) to drive a fade-in/fade-out `resultFlash`, then used `lastSaved` for the first time — a save now gets an expanding golden glow ring around the ball (visually "secured it"), and a goal now bulges the net cords outward near the impact point (top pinned to crossbar, bottom shifts via a gaussian falloff from the ball's x, so it reads as a real net-catches-the-ball moment, not just the ball vanishing). Zero gameplay/mechanics change — purely a rendering fix that finally uses state the game was already computing. PRIOR_BATCH_115:   honest-repass-batch-115. Finished the canvas-only-HUD accessibility lens (queued by batches 113/114). Fixed `firefly_count_game.dart`: its tappable multiple-choice answer NUMBERS were drawn only onto the canvas with no Semantics at all, so a screen-reader user couldn't even discover what the choices were (same "unplayable blind" severity class as the Slide Puzzle/Dot-to-Dot fixes in batch 114). Added invisible, independently-tappable `Semantics` overlays mirroring the painter's three pad rects, each also firing the same `_pick` handler as the canvas tap. Also mirrored the remaining 5 canvas-only onboarding hints (these sensory toys have no shared shell/banner to mirror into, unlike arcade games) into invisible `Positioned.fill(child: IgnorePointer(child: Semantics(label: ...)))` overlays layered over each `CustomPaint` via a `Stack` (IgnorePointer keeps the label-only overlay from stealing touches from the real gesture handler): `KaleidoscopeToy` ("Draw — it mirrors into a kaleidoscope"), `FluidSimulatorToy` ("Swirl your finger to stir the colors") in `toys_draw.dart`; `PaintWithLightToy` ("Draw with your finger") in `toys_light.dart`; `CarTrackBuilderToy` ("Draw a road with your finger") in `toys_more.dart`; `FireworksToy` ("Tap to launch a firework") in `toys_particles.dart`. (Note: the file:line pairing in batch 114's NEXT_ACTION for the last 4 hints didn't exactly match current file layout — fixed by searching for the actual onboarding-hint strings instead and confirmed all 5 found this way.) This closes the canvas-only-HUD lens catalog-wide — no more TextPainter-drawn interactive/onboarding text lacking a Semantics equivalent. Zero visual change — purely additive semantics (had to drop a stray `const` on the new `Positioned.fill(...)` wrappers since `Semantics` isn't a const constructor). Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: a0fedc7.
PRIOR_BATCH_114:   honest-repass-batch-114. Ran the "canvas-only HUD text invisible to screen readers" lens (candidate 3 queued by batch 113) as a full dedicated sweep via background explore agent across every `TextPainter` usage in every arcade file + the shared toy files, since the batch-112/113 shell-level fixes only covered score/banner/message widget-tree text, not text drawn directly onto a `Canvas` (invisible to the accessibility tree by construction). Found 10 genuine gaps; fixed the two highest-value, most-functional ones: `SlidePuzzleGame` and `DotToDotGame` paint their ENTIRE interactive board state (which number sits in which of the 9 tiles; which numbered dot is which) only onto canvas pixels, so a screen-reader user had literally no way to learn the puzzle state or find a specific dot/tile — not just a cosmetic gap but a "the game is unplayable blind" gap. Added invisible, independently-tappable `Semantics` overlays (`Positioned` + `SizedBox.expand()` per cell/dot) so TalkBack/VoiceOver touch-exploration announces "Tile 5" / "Dot 3, tap next" etc. at the right on-screen position, and each overlay also fires the same tap handler as the canvas gesture. Also fixed the two lower-effort onboarding-hint gaps the same sweep found: Brick Break's "Drag to move · release the ball" and Sky Hop's "Tap to flap" were painted only on canvas pre-first-input; mirrored both into their shared shell's already-`liveRegion`-announced banner. Six remaining candidates (firefly_count's answer-choice numbers, toys_draw/toys_light/toys_more/toys_particles' sensory-toy onboarding hints) were left for a future batch — closed by batch 115. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 949d93a.
PRIOR_BATCH_111:   honest-repass-batch-111. Ran the `universe_catalog.dart` inputs-metadata-vs-mechanics lens as a full dedicated sweep across all 78 Play games (via background explore agent), cross-checking every declared `inputs: const [ToyInput...]` list against each game's real gesture-handling code. Found + fixed one real bug: Pinball declared `[ToyInput.tap]` but has NO tap handling at all — its flippers are raised via a `Listener`'s onPointerDown/onPointerUp tracking per-pointer-id held sets (`_leftHeldPointers`/`_rightHeldPointers`), deliberately built so two fingers can hold both flippers simultaneously (the game's own code comments explain why a tap recognizer would drop a genuine two-finger flip). Corrected to `[ToyInput.hold, ToyInput.multiTouch]`. Two other candidates the agent flagged (`snack_studio`/`echo` both declaring `tap+sound`) were investigated and confirmed NOT bugs: `ToyInput.sound` is already used catalog-wide (7 other toys: pop_it-style keypad/click_pen/fireworks/music_garden/etc.) to mean "has audio feedback as part of its identity," not "requires microphone/voice input" — there is no mic/voice-input mechanic anywhere in this app, so that label's established meaning is output-audio, not input. This closes the inputs-metadata lens as a dedicated full-catalog sweep — only Pinball was a genuine mismatch. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: e610aa2.
PRIOR_BATCH_112:   honest-repass-batch-112. Ran the accessibility screen-reader lens (candidate 2 queued by batch 111) across the score-pill/game-over-banner chokepoint. Found + fixed a real catalog-wide gap: every one of the 78 Play games renders its score pill and win/lose overlay through one of three near-identical shared shell widgets (`_Shell` in `arcade_games.dart`, `_GameShell` in `mini_games.dart`, `_GoalShell` in `goal_games.dart`), and none of them exposed anything to TalkBack/VoiceOver beyond default static-text semantics — a score climbing mid-run was silent to a screen-reader user, and the win/lose overlay appearing (a same-route conditional Stack swap, not a new route) was never announced, only discoverable by manually swiping around. Fixed all three shells: wrapped each score pill in `Semantics(liveRegion: true)` so assistive tech re-announces it as the score changes, and added an invisible one-shot `Semantics(liveRegion: true, label: ...)` announcer (a `SizedBox.shrink()`, placed before the existing visible Text nodes so it doesn't steal focus from the still-individually-tappable Play-again/Back-to-games buttons) summarizing win/lose text + score + best + new-best badge (`_GoalShell` additionally folds in its star-rating text). Zero visual change — purely additive semantics. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: cfa0f49.
CURRENT_GAME:      Batch 113: extended the same screen-reader lens (candidate 1 queued by batch 112) to the shared shells' OTHER live-updating pill — the `banner` param in `_Shell`/`_GameShell` and the `message` param in `_GoalShell`. This pill is reused by 25+ games (confirmed via a targeted grep of every `banner:`/`message:` call site) for live mid-run status via setState — lives remaining (often rendered as bespoke repeated-emoji counts like `'💛' * _lives` or `'🧤' * _lives`), round/level progress, and terminal "Out of lives!" text — but unlike the score pill it had NO semantics at all beyond default static-text, so a life lost or a level advancing was completely silent to a screen-reader user with no way to even manually re-discover the change short of losing focus and re-finding the pill. Fixed by wrapping all three shells' banner/message pill in `Semantics(container: true, liveRegion: true)`, mirroring the exact pattern already proven correct for the score pill in batch 112. Investigated the START/intro card (other half of candidate 1): its title/introHow/Play button already get adequate default Text/button semantics, and the intro card's own decorative icon `Text(title.split(' ').first)` is a harmless duplicate read (the full `title` text immediately below it already contains the same emoji + name) — not worth touching since removing it from the accessibility tree risks losing the one piece of pre-game content a screen-reader user currently gets for free; left alone as a non-bug. Zero visual change — purely additive semantics. Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 163/163 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: dad0d71.

Found one genuine, real bug outside that initial list while cross-checking `memory_flip_game.dart` (queued from batch 89's never-named sweep): its `_deal()` always built each level's icon set via `_facePool.take(_pairsThisLevel)` — a fixed front-of-list slice — so level 1 of EVERY playthrough forever used the exact same first 6 icons (🍎⭐🐢🎈🌸🚗), level 2 the same first 7, etc. Only card *position* was shuffled, never *which* icons appeared — the same "identical every session" gap class already fixed in `piano_song`/`dot_to_dot`/`mirror_draw`. Fixed by shuffling a copy of `_facePool` before taking `_pairsThisLevel` icons, so each level's icon set is freshly randomized per deal. It surfaced a genuine 10th instance in `brick_break_game.dart`: the ball is painted as a true pixel circle whose radius is derived from canvas width alone (`ballR * w` in `_BrickPainter`), but every y-axis collision margin (top wall, paddle, brick top/bottom) compared the ball's height-normalized y-position against that same width-normalized `_ballR` directly, with no aspect correction anywhere in the file (verified via a direct grep — no `_aspect` field existed at all, unlike the already-fixed mini_golf/pinball/air_hockey/basketball/maze_marble). On a typical non-square phone this stretched the vertical hitbox relative to the drawn ball, so the ball could visibly bounce off bricks/the paddle while still looking clearly separated on screen (or, with a squashed vertical margin on the opposite aspect, require a deeper-than-drawn overlap before counting as a hit) — a real, child-visible fairness/feel bug in one of CLAUDE.md's explicitly flagged high-risk games. Fixed with the now-established pattern: added an `_aspect` field (height/width) tracked from the existing `LayoutBuilder`, and a `ballRY = _ballR / _aspect` conversion applied to the top-wall, paddle, and brick y-margins (x-axis margins were already correct and left untouched, since the ball's pixel radius is width-based). Verified: full `flutter test --no-pub` 161/161 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 590e993.

Reading it end-to-end exposed a real feel/fairness weakness even though the gestures were correct: every run and every new level began immediately under a live countdown with all cards face-down, so the first interaction was memorization-by-random-poking rather than a genuine memory challenge, especially after the higher-level grid expansions. Fixed by adding a 1.4s auto-preview at game start, replay, and after each level transition: all cards now flip face-up briefly, the timer pauses during the preview, taps are ignored until it ends, and the banner clearly says "Memorize the pairs!" before the timed play starts. This keeps the intended pressure/reward loop (time banking still matters once play begins) while making the actual memory mechanic fairer and easier to parse in the first seconds. PRIOR_BATCH_73:    honest-repass-batch-73. Found two real, distinct bugs in the other two: (1) `trace_game.dart`'s `_drag` computed `(_pts[k] - p).distance` where both `_pts[k]` and `p` are normalized (dx/width, dy/height) coordinates — Offset.distance is an isotropic sqrt, so on any phone where width != height this is the same aspect-ratio hit-test distortion bug class fixed six times already (space_dodge/batch 58, count_pop/63, balloon_math/64, maze_marble/65, pinball+air_hockey+mini_golf+basketball/66, steady_hand/67, letter_trace/72), here on the fine-motor tracing dot-lighting check: the painter draws every dot as a true fixed-pixel circle (9-16px radius) at `pts[i].dx*w, pts[i].dy*h`, so the hit-test comparing normalized distance against a flat `0.06` threshold stretched the catch radius unevenly between axes on any non-square canvas. Fixed by comparing real pixel distance (`dot - local`, both in true screen pixels) against a fixed 32px radius instead, dropping the normalized-distance approach entirely rather than threading an aspect correction through it — simpler and more directly matches the drawn geometry. (2) `block_blast_game.dart`'s intro instructions read "Drag the blocks onto the grid" but the actual implemented mechanic is tap-to-select a hand slot (`_sel`) then tap-to-place on a grid cell (`onTapDown: (_) => _place(r, c)`) — there is no `Draggable`/`DragTarget` or pan gesture anywhere in the file. This is a genuine first-interaction claim-vs-reality mismatch (CLAUDE.md section 3's "first interaction" criterion): a child reading "drag" and trying to actually drag a block would get no visual feedback and conclude the game is broken, when the real mechanic (tap then tap) works fine once discovered. Fixed by rewriting the instruction text to describe the real mechanic ("Tap a block below to pick it, then tap the grid to place it!") rather than attempting to add real drag support, which would be a much larger, riskier rewrite than the actual product gap (unclear instructions) warrants. Verified: `flutter test --no-pub` 161/161 passed; `flutter analyze --no-pub` 0 warnings / 0 errors (115 info, unchanged baseline bucket). Commit: 17fa970.

PRIOR_BATCH_67:    honest-repass-batch-67. Took batch 66's NEXT_ACTION option (3): read the two remaining not-yet-individually-read arcade files, `skee_ball_game.dart` and `penalty_dash_game.dart`, end-to-end — both genuinely clean (skee_ball's ball position is a single vertical scalar with ring bands checked as 1-D thresholds; penalty_dash's marker/keeper/posts are all pure 1-D horizontal positions), confirming the aspect-ratio bug class really is exhausted across the whole 68-file arcade roster now. Found a real, more serious bug in `steady_hand_game.dart`: the path corridor is drawn as a true-pixel stroked band (`strokeWidth = halfW * 2 * w`, isotropic in real screen pixels, by Flutter's own stroke semantics), but `_distToSeg` measured the finger's drift from the corridor centreline using a plain isotropic `sqrt(dx*dx+dy*dy)` in mixed-normalized space (x as a fraction of canvas width, y as a fraction of canvas height) and compared it straight against the width-calibrated `_halfW` — the exact aspect-ratio bug class from batches 58/63/64/65/66, but here governing the core pass/fail wall-touch check for the entire game, not just a hit-test. On a typical taller-than-wide phone this under-tolerated drift on vertically-oriented path segments and over-tolerated it on horizontally-oriented ones, so a child's finger could visibly clip a horizontal wall section without failing, or fail on a vertical section while still looking inside the drawn corridor, depending purely on that segment's orientation. The same calibration mismatch was also present in the simpler per-axis box checks for grabbing the start dot and detecting the finish (both used `_halfW` directly as the y-threshold too). Fixed by threading the live `aspect` (height/width, already available in the existing `LayoutBuilder`) through `_distToPath`/`_distToSeg` (scaling the y-axis before the distance calculation, so it's compared correctly against the width-calibrated `_halfW` regardless of segment direction) and by dividing the y-threshold by `aspect` in both the start-grab and finish-reach box checks for the same reason.
